3. **`crates/web/Cargo.toml`** -- Merged both: kept HEAD's `moltis-httpd` dependency (from the httpd extraction) plus main's alignment formatting for all other deps.

4. **`crates/web/src/assets/js/app.js`** -- Merged both: kept HEAD's `?include_sessions=false` query param AND main's 401/403 auth error handling in the bootstrap fetch.

5. **`crates/web/src/assets/js/components/session-header.js`** -- Took HEAD's `!(isMain || isCron)` guard for the delete button (more restrictive than main's `!isMain`), plus all the shared button markup from both sides.

7. **`crates/web/src/assets/js/page-nodes.js`** -- Merged both: kept HEAD's `gon` import and gon-based port resolution in `gatewayWsUrl()`, combined with main's `moltis node add` command string (matching the new `Run` subcommand architecture).

8. **`crates/web/src/templates.rs`** -- Took main's version (superset): includes all of HEAD's mem snapshot tests plus main's new `setup_required_template_renders_html` and `onboarding_redirect_waits_for_auth_recovery` tests.

## Summary of Resolved Conflicts

### 1. `.github/workflows/release.yml` (4 conflicts)
- All conflicts were about the WASM precompile step: both sides added `cargo run -p moltis-wasm-precompile --release` and the "Build release binary" step. Kept both (precompile + build binary). The Windows variant kept HEAD's `--features embedded-assets,embedded-wasm` flag.

### 2. `crates/agents/src/prompt.rs` (2 conflicts)
- **Exec routing guidance**: origin/main added sandbox-aware routing (`EXEC_ROUTING_GUIDANCE_SANDBOX`/`EXEC_ROUTING_GUIDANCE_HOST_ONLY`, sudo hints, sandbox closing). Kept origin/main's more complete version, which also includes the `NODE_ROUTING_GUIDANCE` from HEAD.
- **Test fixture**: Changed `sandbox: Some(...)` to `sandbox: None` to match origin/main's sandbox-aware routing logic.

### 3. `crates/browser/src/pool.rs` (2 conflicts)
- Used origin/main's `MAX_BROWSER_INSTANCE_LIFETIME` constant (defined at file top) instead of HEAD's inline `MAX_LIFETIME`. Used origin/main's rustdoc link in the doc comment.

### 4. `crates/config/src/lib.rs` (1 conflict)
- Merged both sides' re-exports: HEAD's `share_dir`/`set_share_dir`/`clear_share_dir` + origin/main's `save_soul_for_agent`.

### 5. `crates/config/src/loader.rs` (1 conflict)
- Kept both sides' test additions: origin/main's `save_soul_for_agent_*` tests + HEAD's `share_dir_*` tests.

### 6. `crates/gateway/Cargo.toml` (1 conflict)
- Kept HEAD's `tls = []` (empty feature, deps extracted to httpd crate) over origin/main's dep list.

### 7. `crates/gateway/src/server.rs` (many conflicts)
- Kept HEAD's transport extraction: deleted `AppState`, `build_cors_layer`, `build_gateway_base`, `build_gateway_app`, `finalize_gateway_app`, `prepare_gateway_embedded`, `start_gateway` (all moved to `crates/httpd/`).
- Incorporated origin/main's new additions: `maybe_deliver_cron_output` function, `restore_saved_local_llm_models` function, `_proxy_shutdown_tx` field, `browser_tool_for_warmup` field, rustls CryptoProvider early install, `StartupMemProbe`, background model discovery with `restore_saved_local_llm_models` call, `Secret::new` (shorter import).
- Fixed `PreparedGatewayCore` struct: consolidated all fields properly (was split across the struct and a phantom `BannerMeta` due to conflict overlap).

### Note
- Origin/main added a `/ws` route alias (in addition to `/ws/chat`) in the `build_gateway_base` function. Since that code was extracted to `crates/httpd/src/server.rs`, the `/ws` route should be added there separately.

    27→/// A mock cron service that stores jobs in memory for testing.
    28→struct MockCronService {
    29→    jobs: Arc<Mutex<HashMap<String, CronJob>>>,
    30→}
    31→
    32→impl MockCronService {
    33→    fn new() -> Self {
    34→        Self {
    35→            jobs: Arc::new(Mutex::new(HashMap::new())),
    36→        }
    37→    }
    38→
    39→    fn get_job(&self, name: &str) -> Option<CronJob> {
    40→        self.jobs
    41→            .lock()
    42→            .unwrap()
    43→            .values()
    44→            .find(|j| j.name == name)
    45→            .cloned()
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    #[allow(dead_code)]
    49→    fn job_count(&self) -> usize {
    50→        self.jobs.lock().unwrap().len()
    51→    }
    52→}
    53→
    54→#[async_trait]
    55→impl CronService for MockCronService {
    56→    async fn list(&self) -> ServiceResult {
    57→        let jobs: Vec<CronJob> = self.jobs.lock().unwrap().values().cloned().collect();
    58→        Ok(serde_json::to_value(jobs)?)
    59→    }
    60→
    61→    async fn status(&self) -> ServiceResult {
    62→        Ok(serde_json::json!({ "running": true }))
    63→    }
    64→
    65→    async fn add(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult {
    66→        let create: CronJobCreate =
    67→            serde_json::from_value(params).map_err(|e| format!("invalid job spec: {e}"))?;
    68→
    69→        let job = CronJob {
    70→            id: create
    71→                .id
    72→                .clone()
    73→                .unwrap_or_else(|| uuid::Uuid::new_v4().to_string()),
    74→            name: create.name.clone(),
    75→            schedule: create.schedule.clone(),
    76→            payload: create.payload.clone(),
    77→            session_target: create.session_target.clone(),
    78→            delete_after_run: create.delete_after_run,
    79→            enabled: create.enabled,
    80→            system: create.system,
    81→            sandbox: create.sandbox.clone(),
    82→            wake_mode: create.wake_mode,
    83→            state: moltis_cron::types::CronJobState::default(),
    84→            created_at_ms: 0,
    85→            updated_at_ms: 0,
    86→        };
    87→
    88→        let id = job.id.clone();
    89→        self.jobs.lock().unwrap().insert(id.clone(), job);
    90→
    91→        // Return the created job
    92→        let created = self.jobs.lock().unwrap().get(&id).cloned().unwrap();
    93→        Ok(serde_json::to_value(created)?)
    94→    }
    95→
    96→    async fn update(&self, params: Value) -> ServiceResult {
    97→        let id = params
    98→            .get("id")
    99→            .and_then(|v| v.as_str())
   100→            .ok_or_else(|| "missing 'id'".to_string())?;
   101→        let patch: CronJobPatch = params
   102→            .get("patch")
   103→            .and_then(|v| serde_json::from_value(v.clone()).ok())
   104→            .ok_or_else(|| "missing or invalid 'patch'".to_string())?;
   105→
   106→        let mut jobs = self.jobs.lock().unwrap();
   107→        if let Some(job) = jobs.get_mut(id) {
   108→            // Apply patches to the job
   109→            if let Some(name) = patch.name {
   110→                job.name = name;
   111→            }
   112→            if let Some(schedule) = patch.schedule {
   113→                job.schedule = schedule;
   114→            }
   115→            if let Some(payload) = patch.payload {

   117→            }
   118→            if let Some(session_target) = patch.session_target {

   120→            }
   121→            if let Some(enabled) = patch.enabled {

   123→            }
   124→            if let Some(delete_after_run) = patch.delete_after_run {

   126→            }
   127→            if let Some(sandbox) = patch.sandbox {
   128→                job.sandbox = sandbox;
   129→            }
   130→            if let Some(wake_mode) = patch.wake_mode {

   134→        } else {

   166→/// Start a test server with a mock cron service.
   167→async fn start_test_server_with_mock_cron() -> (SocketAddr, Arc<MockCronService>) {
   168→    let mock_cron = Arc::new(MockCronService::new());
   169→    let resolved_auth = auth::resolve_auth(None, None);
   170→
   171→    let services = GatewayServices::noop().with_cron(mock_cron.clone());
   172→
   173→    let state = GatewayState::new(resolved_auth, services);
   174→    let methods = Arc::new(MethodRegistry::new());
   175→
   176→    #[cfg(feature = "push-notifications")]
   177→    let (router, app_state) = build_gateway_base(state, methods, None, None);
   178→    #[cfg(not(feature = "push-notifications"))]
   179→    let (router, app_state) = build_gateway_base(state, methods, None);
   180→
   181→    let router = router.merge(moltis_web::web_routes());
   182→    let app = finalize_gateway_app(router, app_state, false);
   183→
   184→    let listener = TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").await.unwrap();
   185→    let addr = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
   186→
   187→    tokio::spawn(async move {
   188→        axum::serve(
   189→            listener,
   190→            app.into_make_service_with_connect_info::<SocketAddr>(),
   191→        )
   192→        .await
   193→        .unwrap();
   194→    });
   195→
   196→    (addr, mock_cron)
   197→}
   198→
   199→#[tokio::test]
   200→async fn heartbeat_update_creates_cron_job_when_prompt_configured() {
   201→    let (addr, mock_cron) = start_test_server_with_mock_cron().await;
   202→
   203→    // Initially, no heartbeat job should exist
   204→    assert!(mock_cron.get_job("__heartbeat__").is_none());
   205→
   206→    // Connect via WebSocket
   207→    let (mut ws, _) = tokio_tungstenite::connect_async(format!("ws://{addr}/ws/chat"))
   208→        .await
   209→        .expect("ws connect failed");
   210→
   211→    // Send connect handshake
   212→    let connect_frame = serde_json::json!({
   213→        "type": "req",
   214→        "id": "test-connect",
   215→        "method": "connect",
   216→        "params": {
   217→            "minProtocol": 3,
   218→            "maxProtocol": 4,
   219→            "client": {
   220→                "id": "test-client",
   221→                "version": "0.0.1",
   222→                "platform": "test",
   223→                "mode": "operator"
   224→            }
   225→        }
   226→    });
   227→    ws.send(tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message::Text(
   228→        connect_frame.to_string().into(),
   229→    ))
   230→    .await
   231→    .unwrap();
   232→
   233→    // Consume hello-ok
   234→    let _ = ws.next().await.unwrap().unwrap();
   235→
   236→    // Call heartbeat.update with a custom prompt
   237→    let heartbeat_update = serde_json::json!({
   238→        "type": "req",
   239→        "id": "hb-update-1",
   240→        "method": "heartbeat.update",
   241→        "params": {
   242→            "enabled": true,
   243→            "every": "30m",
   244→            "prompt": "Test heartbeat prompt",
   245→            "model": null,
   246→            "ackMaxChars": 500,
   247→            "activeHours": {
   248→                "start": "00:00",
   249→                "end": "23:59"
   250→            },
   251→            "deliver": false,
   252→            "channel": null,
   253→            "to": null,
   254→            "sandboxEnabled": false,
   255→            "sandboxImage": null
   256→        }
   257→    });
   258→    ws.send(tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message::Text(
   259→        heartbeat_update.to_string().into(),
   260→    ))
   261→    .await
   262→    .unwrap();
   263→
   264→    // Read the response
   265→    let msg = ws.next().await.unwrap().unwrap();
   266→    let frame: Value = serde_json::from_str(msg.to_text().unwrap()).unwrap();
   267→
   268→    // Should succeed
   269→    assert_eq!(frame["type"], "res");
   270→    assert_eq!(frame["id"], "hb-update-1");
   271→    assert_eq!(
   272→        frame["ok"],
   273→        true,
   274→        "heartbeat.update failed: {:?}",
   275→        frame.get("payload")
   276→    );
   277→
   278→    // Now the heartbeat job should exist
   279→    let job = mock_cron.get_job("__heartbeat__");
   280→    assert!(job.is_some(), "heartbeat cron job should have been created");
   281→
   282→    let job = job.unwrap();
   283→    assert_eq!(job.name, "__heartbeat__");
   284→    assert!(job.system);
   285→
   286→    ws.close(None).await.ok();
   287→}

   372→#[tokio::test]
   373→async fn heartbeat_update_updates_existing_job() {
   374→    let (addr, mock_cron) = start_test_server_with_mock_cron().await;
   375→
   376→    // Pre-create a heartbeat job using the Rust struct directly
   377→    let create = CronJobCreate {
   378→        id: Some("__heartbeat__".into()),
   379→        name: "__heartbeat__".into(),
   380→        schedule: moltis_cron::types::CronSchedule::Every {
   381→            every_ms: 1800000,
   382→            anchor_ms: None,
   383→        },
   384→        payload: moltis_cron::types::CronPayload::AgentTurn {
   385→            message: "Old prompt".into(),
   386→            model: None,
   387→            timeout_secs: None,
   388→            deliver: false,
   389→            channel: None,
   390→            to: None,
   391→        },
   392→        session_target: moltis_cron::types::SessionTarget::Named("heartbeat".into()),
   393→        delete_after_run: false,
   394→        enabled: true,
   395→        system: true,
   396→        sandbox: moltis_cron::types::CronSandboxConfig {
   397→            enabled: false,
   398→            image: None,
   399→        },
   400→        wake_mode: moltis_cron::types::CronWakeMode::NextHeartbeat,
   401→    };
   402→    let create_json = serde_json::to_value(create).unwrap();
   403→    mock_cron.add(create_json).await.unwrap();
   404→
   405→    // Verify initial state
   406→    let initial_job = mock_cron.get_job("__heartbeat__").unwrap();
   407→    assert!(initial_job.enabled);
   408→    // Check that the job has the old prompt
   409→    match &initial_job.payload {
   410→        moltis_cron::types::CronPayload::AgentTurn { message, .. } => {
   411→            assert_eq!(message, "Old prompt");
   412→        },
   413→        _ => panic!("Expected AgentTurn payload"),
   414→    }
   415→
   416→    // Connect via WebSocket
   417→    let (mut ws, _) = tokio_tungstenite::connect_async(format!("ws://{addr}/ws/chat"))
   418→        .await
   419→        .expect("ws connect failed");
   420→
   421→    // Send connect handshake
   422→    let connect_frame = serde_json::json!({
   423→        "type": "req",
   424→        "id": "test-connect",
   425→        "method": "connect",
   426→        "params": {
   427→            "minProtocol": 3,
   428→            "maxProtocol": 4,
   429→            "client": {
   430→                "id": "test-client",
   431→                "version": "0.0.1",
   432→                "platform": "test",
   433→                "mode": "operator"
   434→            }
   435→        }
   436→    });
   437→    ws.send(tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message::Text(
   438→        connect_frame.to_string().into(),
   439→    ))
   440→    .await
   441→    .unwrap();
   442→
   443→    // Consume hello-ok
   444→    let _ = ws.next().await.unwrap().unwrap();
   445→
   446→    // Call heartbeat.update to update the existing job
   447→    let heartbeat_update = serde_json::json!({
   448→        "type": "req",
   449→        "id": "hb-update-1",
   450→        "method": "heartbeat.update",
   451→        "params": {
   452→            "enabled": false,
   453→            "every": "60m",
   454→            "prompt": "Updated prompt",
   455→            "model": null,
   456→            "ackMaxChars": 500,
   457→            "activeHours": {
   458→                "start": "09:00",
   459→                "end": "17:00"
   460→            },
   461→            "deliver": false,
   462→            "channel": null,
   463→            "to": null,
   464→            "sandboxEnabled": false,
   465→            "sandboxImage": null
   466→        }
   467→    });
   468→    ws.send(tokio_tungstenite::tungstenite::Message::Text(
   469→        heartbeat_update.to_string().into(),
   470→    ))
   471→    .await
   472→    .unwrap();
   473→
   474→    // Read the response
   475→    let msg = ws.next().await.unwrap().unwrap();
   476→    let frame: Value = serde_json::from_str(msg.to_text().unwrap()).unwrap();
   477→
   478→    // Should succeed
   479→    assert_eq!(frame["type"], "res");
   480→    assert_eq!(frame["id"], "hb-update-1");
   481→    assert_eq!(
   482→        frame["ok"],
   483→        true,
   484→        "heartbeat.update failed: {:?}",
   485→        frame.get("payload")
   486→    );
   487→
   488→    // Verify the job was updated (not created anew)
   489→    let updated_job = mock_cron.get_job("__heartbeat__").unwrap();
   490→    assert!(!updated_job.enabled);
   491→    // Check that the job has the updated prompt
   492→    match &updated_job.payload {
   493→        moltis_cron::types::CronPayload::AgentTurn { message, .. } => {
   494→            assert_eq!(message, "Updated prompt");
   495→        },
   496→        _ => panic!("Expected AgentTurn payload"),
   497→    }
   498→
   499→    ws.close(None).await.ok();
   500→}

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:1533
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P0" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p0.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`rustls` crypto provider not installed for `prepare_httpd_embedded` path**

The `rustls::crypto::ring::default_provider().install_default()` call was moved from `prepare_gateway_core` (where all entry points share it) into `start_gateway` only. The swift-bridge path (`prepare_httpd_embedded` → `prepare_gateway_embedded` → `prepare_gateway` → `prepare_gateway_core`) now **skips this call entirely**.

The original comment from the gateway explicitly documents the risk (see commit history for issue #329):
> Without this, `--no-tls` deployments skip the TLS cert setup path where `install_default()` previously lived, causing a panic the first time an outbound HTTPS request is made.

On the macOS app, the swift-bridge calls `prepare_httpd_embedded` with `no_tls = true`. When channel plugins (Slack, Discord, etc.) subsequently make outbound HTTPS connections, `rustls` will panic because no crypto provider has been installed.

The fix is to call `install_default()` inside `prepare_gateway_embedded` (or `prepare_gateway`) rather than only in `start_gateway`:

```rust
pub async fn prepare_gateway_embedded(
    ...
) -> anyhow::Result<PreparedGateway> {
    // Install rustls provider here too, before channel plugins start
    #[cfg(feature = "tls")]
    let _ = rustls::crypto::ring::default_provider().install_default();

    let prepared = prepare_gateway(...).await?;
    ...
}
```

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:694
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`browser_tool_for_warmup` silently dropped — browser tool warmup disabled**

`PreparedGatewayCore.browser_tool_for_warmup` is populated in `prepare_gateway_core` (set to `shared_tool_registry.read().await.get_arc("browser")`) but is silently dropped by the `..` pattern when destructuring `core` in `prepare_gateway`.

`start_browser_warmup_after_listener` always passes `None` for the tool:

```rust
pub fn start_browser_warmup_after_listener(
    browser_service: Arc<dyn crate::services::BrowserService>,
) {
    spawn_post_listener_warmups(browser_service, None); // tool is always None
}
```

Before this refactor, the old `prepare_gateway` passed the actual tool to `spawn_post_listener_warmups(browser_service, browser_tool_for_warmup)`. The tool's `warmup()` call is now silently skipped every time, even when a browser tool is registered.

To restore the original behavior, expose `browser_tool_for_warmup` via the banner or pass it explicitly to `start_browser_warmup_after_listener`.

---
FILE: .github/workflows/release.yml:481
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Redundant pre-version "Build release binary" steps double CI build time**

Three new "Build release binary" steps (this one and the two analogous ones in the other Linux/macOS jobs around lines 590 and 787) are added *before* "Determine package version". However, an existing "Build release binary" step already runs *after* "Determine package version" with the same flags plus `MOLTIS_VERSION`. The final packaged binary is always the one from the post-version build, so the pre-version builds compile the entire workspace a second time without contributing to the release artifact.

For the Windows job (line ~976), the same pattern applies but both steps use `--features embedded-assets,embedded-wasm`.

These pre-version build steps should either be removed (the post-version build is sufficient) or the intent should be clarified with a comment if they serve a specific purpose (e.g., early compilation error detection).
```

   801→    if !env_flag_enabled("MOLTIS_BROWSER_WARMUP") {
   802→        debug!("startup browser warmup disabled (set MOLTIS_BROWSER_WARMUP=1 to enable)");
   803→        return;
   804→    }
   805→
   806→    tokio::spawn(async move {
   807→        browser_service.warmup().await;
   808→        if let Some(tool) = browser_tool
   809→            && let Err(error) = tool.warmup().await
   810→        {
   811→            warn!(%error, "browser tool warmup failed");
   812→        }
   813→    });
   814→}
   815→
   816→/// Start browser warmup after the transport listener is ready.
   817→pub fn start_browser_warmup_after_listener(
   818→    browser_service: Arc<dyn crate::services::BrowserService>,
   819→) {
   820→    spawn_post_listener_warmups(browser_service, None);
   821→}
   822→
   823→#[cfg(feature = "tailscale")]
   824→fn spawn_webauthn_tailscale_registration(
   825→    registry: SharedWebAuthnRegistry,
   826→    default_scheme: String,
```

> AGENT

All three greptile findings fixed:

- **P0**: `rustls::crypto::ring::default_provider().install_default()` moved from `start_gateway` to `prepare_gateway`, so the swift-bridge/embedded path also gets it (prevents panic on outbound HTTPS from channel plugins)
- **P1**: `browser_tool_for_warmup` extracted from the `..` destructure pattern, threaded through `BannerMeta`, and passed to `start_browser_warmup_after_listener` — restoring browser tool warmup
- **P2**: Removed 4 redundant pre-version build steps across all release jobs (Linux pkg.tar.zst, AppImage, macOS/generic, Windows), cutting unnecessary double-compilation

> AGENT

`git-filter-repo` isn't installed. You can install it and run:

```bash
brew install git-filter-repo
git filter-repo --path logs.txt --invert-paths --force
git push --force
```

Or without installing anything, use `git rebase`:

```bash
git rebase -i --root --exec 'git rm --cached --ignore-unmatch logs.txt'
```

But since only 3 commits touched it, the simplest approach with what you have:

```bash
git filter-branch --force --index-filter \
  'git rm --cached --ignore-unmatch logs.txt' \
  --prune-empty -- --all
git push --force
```

Want me to install `git-filter-repo` via brew and run it? That's the recommended tool (faster, safer than `filter-branch`).

refactor(httpd): extract moltis-httpd crate as HTTP transport facade
## Summary

- Introduce `moltis-httpd` crate to decouple the HTTP/WS transport layer from `moltis-gateway` core business logic, so non-HTTP consumers (TUI, tests) can depend on `moltis-gateway` without pulling in the full HTTP stack
- Extract `PreparedGatewayCore` struct and `prepare_gateway_core()` in gateway to cleanly separate transport-agnostic initialization from HTTP-specific setup

## Summary

- Introduce `moltis-httpd` crate to decouple the HTTP/WS transport layer from `moltis-gateway` core business logic, so non-HTTP consumers (TUI, tests) can depend on `moltis-gateway` without pulling in the full HTTP stack
- Extract `PreparedGatewayCore` struct and `prepare_gateway_core()` in gateway to cleanly separate transport-agnostic initialization from HTTP-specific setup
- Update CLI, web, and swift-bridge crates to import HTTP types (`AppState`, `RouteEnhancer`, route modules, middleware) from `moltis_httpd` instead of `moltis_gateway::server`

This is **Phase 1** (re-export facade): HTTP modules remain in gateway while httpd re-exports them, giving consumers a stable import path. The actual file migration will follow in a future step.

## Validation

### Completed
- [x] `cargo check --workspace` — clean, no warnings
- [x] `cargo test --workspace` — all tests pass (0 failures)
- [x] `cargo test -p moltis-gateway` — 283 tests pass
- [x] `cargo +nightly-2025-11-30 fmt --all -- --check` — clean
- [x] `taplo fmt --check` — clean

### Remaining
- [ ] `cargo +nightly-2025-11-30 clippy -Z unstable-options --workspace --all-features --all-targets --timings -- -D warnings`
- [ ] `./scripts/local-validate.sh <PR_NUMBER>`
- [ ] E2E tests: `cd crates/web/ui && npx playwright test`
- [ ] Manual QA: start server, verify WebSocket + web UI work
- [ ] macOS swift-bridge build: `./scripts/build-swift-bridge.sh`

## Manual QA

1. `cargo run` — verify server starts, banner prints correctly
2. Open web UI in browser — verify pages load
3. Check WebSocket connection establishes
4. Verify TLS works (if enabled)

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:556
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P0" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p0.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`_proxy_shutdown_tx` dropped immediately, killing the trusted-network proxy**

When `PreparedGatewayCore` is destructured with `..`, Rust drops the unlisted fields — including `_proxy_shutdown_tx` — at the end of the `let` statement. Since `PreparedGateway` has no corresponding field to hold the sender, the `watch::Sender` is dropped immediately after `prepare_gateway_core` returns. Dropping the sender closes the watch channel, which is the trusted-network proxy's shutdown signal, causing it to terminate the moment the gateway finishes initialising.

The previous architecture stored `_proxy_shutdown_tx` in `PreparedGateway` (see the old `PreparedGateway` definition) precisely to keep the proxy alive for the server's full lifetime. `PreparedGateway` in `moltis-httpd` should include a similar field:

```rust
#[cfg(feature = "trusted-network")]
_proxy_shutdown_tx: Option<tokio::sync::watch::Sender<bool>>,
```

and the struct construction at line 1485 must capture it from the destructured `core`.

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:994
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Session WebSocket events missing `entry` metadata — real-time session list updates broken**

The old session event forwarder (in `gateway/src/server.rs`) enriched each broadcast with a full `entry` object:

```json
{
  "kind": "created",
  "sessionKey": "...",
  "entry": {
    "id": "...", "label": "...", "model": "...", "createdAt": ...,
    "messageCount": ..., "preview": "...", "agentId": "...", ...
  }
}
```

The new forwarder only broadcasts `kind` and `sessionKey`. Web-UI clients subscribe to `"session"` events to update their session lists without polling; stripping the `entry` payload means clients learn that a session changed but can't update their state without a full re-fetch, regressing real-time UX (and likely breaking components that read `event.entry` directly).

The enrichment logic from the old `gateway/src/server.rs` (the block that reads from `ws_state.services.session_metadata`, resolves `active_channel`, truncates `preview`, etc.) needs to be ported into this spawned task.

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:272
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`/ws` route removed — node-add generated command URL is now broken**

The old `build_gateway_base` registered both `/ws/chat` and `/ws` pointing to `ws_upgrade_handler`. The new code only registers `/ws/chat`.

`crates/web/src/assets/js/page-nodes.js` (a file changed in this PR) still builds the gateway WebSocket URL as `/ws`:

```js
// page-nodes.js line 30
return `${proto}//${host}${port ? `:${port}` : ""}/ws`;
```

This URL is inserted directly into the user-facing command:
```js
command: `moltis node add --host ${wsUrl} --token ${…}`,
```

Any user who runs this generated command after deploying this change will attempt to connect to a `404`-returning endpoint. Either restore the `/ws` alias in `build_gateway_base`, or update `page-nodes.js` to emit `/ws/chat`.

```suggestion
    let mut router = Router::new()
        .route("/health", get(health_handler))
        .route("/ws/chat", get(ws_upgrade_handler))
        .route("/ws", get(ws_upgrade_handler));
```

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:565
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P0" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p0.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`_proxy_shutdown_tx` dropped immediately, killing the trusted-network proxy**

When `PreparedGatewayCore` is destructured with `..`, Rust drops the unlisted fields — including `_proxy_shutdown_tx` — at the end of the `let` statement. Since `PreparedGateway` has no corresponding field to hold the sender, the `watch::Sender` is dropped immediately after `prepare_gateway_core` returns. Dropping the sender closes the watch channel, which is the trusted-network proxy's shutdown signal, causing it to terminate the moment the gateway finishes initialising.

The previous architecture stored `_proxy_shutdown_tx` in `PreparedGateway` (see the old `PreparedGateway` definition) precisely to keep the proxy alive for the server's full lifetime. `PreparedGateway` in `moltis-httpd` should include a similar field:

```rust
#[cfg(feature = "trusted-network")]
_proxy_shutdown_tx: Option<tokio::sync::watch::Sender<bool>>,
```

and the struct construction at line 1485 must capture it from the destructured `core`.

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:1061
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Session WebSocket events missing `entry` metadata — real-time session list updates broken**

The old session event forwarder (in `gateway/src/server.rs`) enriched each broadcast with a full `entry` object:

```json
{
  "kind": "created",
  "sessionKey": "...",
  "entry": {
    "id": "...", "label": "...", "model": "...", "createdAt": ...,
    "messageCount": ..., "preview": "...", "agentId": "...", ...
  }
}
```

The new forwarder only broadcasts `kind` and `sessionKey`. Web-UI clients subscribe to `"session"` events to update their session lists without polling; stripping the `entry` payload means clients learn that a session changed but can't update their state without a full re-fetch, regressing real-time UX (and likely breaking components that read `event.entry` directly).

The enrichment logic from the old `gateway/src/server.rs` (the block that reads from `ws_state.services.session_metadata`, resolves `active_channel`, truncates `preview`, etc.) needs to be ported into this spawned task.

---
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:272
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`/ws` route removed — node-add generated command URL is now broken**

The old `build_gateway_base` registered both `/ws/chat` and `/ws` pointing to `ws_upgrade_handler`. The new code only registers `/ws/chat`.

`crates/web/src/assets/js/page-nodes.js` (a file changed in this PR) still builds the gateway WebSocket URL as `/ws`:

```js
// page-nodes.js line 30
return `${proto}//${host}${port ? `:${port}` : ""}/ws`;
```

This URL is inserted directly into the user-facing command:
```js
command: `moltis node add --host ${wsUrl} --token ${…}`,
```

Any user who runs this generated command after deploying this change will attempt to connect to a `404`-returning endpoint. Either restore the `/ws` alias in `build_gateway_base`, or update `page-nodes.js` to emit `/ws/chat`.

```suggestion
    let mut router = Router::new()
        .route("/health", get(health_handler))
        .route("/ws/chat", get(ws_upgrade_handler))
        .route("/ws", get(ws_upgrade_handler));
```

---
FILE: crates/web/src/lib.rs:157
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P0" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p0.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Duplicate route registrations — axum will panic at startup**

`/api/sessions` (GET) and `/api/sessions/{session_key}/history` (GET) are each registered twice in the same chained `.route()` call. Axum panics with a "Duplicate routes" error when the router is finalized, which means the web server will crash immediately on startup.

The second pair of `.route()` calls (lines 153–157) appears to be a merge artefact — the new `history` route was added and then the original `sessions` route was accidentally left in again alongside it.

```suggestion
        .route("/api/sessions", get(api::api_sessions_handler))
        .route(
            "/api/sessions/{session_key}/history",
            get(api::api_session_history_handler),
        )
```

FILE: crates/node-host/src/service.rs:231
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **XML injection — `xml_escape` removed but still needed**

The `xml_escape` helper was deleted in this PR, but its purpose is still required. Every value now written directly into a plist `<string>` element — `bin`, `log`, `config.gateway_url`, `config.device_token`, `config.node_id`, `config.display_name`, and `config.working_dir` — can contain `&`, `<`, or `>` (e.g. a WebSocket URL such as `ws://host/?a=1&b=2`, or a display name like `Dev & Staging`). Any of those characters will produce malformed XML, and `launchd` will silently refuse to load the plist.

The old test `launchd_plist_escapes_xml_special_chars` explicitly covered this and was removed as part of this change.

The simplest fix is to restore the helper (or use a lightweight XML-escaping crate) and apply it to every interpolated value:

```rust
fn xml_escape(s: &str) -> String {
    s.replace('&', "&amp;")
        .replace('<', "&lt;")
        .replace('>', "&gt;")
        .replace('"', "&quot;")
}
```

and then use it:
```rust
let bin = xml_escape(&moltis_bin.display().to_string());
let log = xml_escape(&log_path.display().to_string());
// ...
format!("    <string>{}</string>", xml_escape(&config.gateway_url)),
format!("    <string>{}</string>", xml_escape(&config.device_token)),
// etc.
```

---
FILE: crates/node-host/src/service.rs:431
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Unquoted values in systemd `ExecStart` — spaces will break unit parsing**

`display_name` and `working_dir` are appended to the `ExecStart` string without quoting. Systemd splits `ExecStart` on whitespace (unless the token is quoted), so a name like `"My Node"` or a directory like `/home/user/my project` would be silently split into multiple arguments at the wrong boundary, causing the service to start with the wrong flags or fail entirely.

The `--node-id` field shares the same issue if it ever contains a space.

Consider quoting each interpolated value:

```rust
if let Some(ref id) = config.node_id {
    exec_args.push_str(&format!(" --node-id \"{}\"", id.replace('"', "\\\"")));
}
if let Some(ref name) = config.display_name {
    exec_args.push_str(&format!(" --name \"{}\"", name.replace('"', "\\\"")));
}
if let Some(ref dir) = config.working_dir {
    exec_args.push_str(&format!(" --working-dir \"{}\"", dir.replace('"', "\\\"")));
}
```

  "new_string": "/// Escape special XML characters for safe interpolation into plist values.\nfn xml_escape(s: &str) -> String {\n    s.replace('&', \"&amp;\")\n        .replace('<', \"&lt;\")\n        .replace('>', \"&gt;\")\n        .replace('\"', \"&quot;\")\n}\n\n/// Generate a launchd plist XML string.\npub fn generate_launchd_plist(\n    moltis_bin: &Path,\n    config: &ServiceConfig,\n    log_path: &Path,\n) -> String {\n    let bin = xml_escape(&moltis_bin.display().to_string());\n    let log = xml_escape(&log_path.display().to_string());\n\n    let mut args = vec![\n        format!(\"    <string>{bin}</string>\"),\n        \"    <string>node</string>\".to_string(),\n        \"    <string>run</string>\".to_string(),\n        format!(\"    <string>--host</string>\"),\n        format!(\"    <string>{}</string>\", xml_escape(&config.gateway_url)),\n        format!(\"    <string>--token</string>\"),\n        format!(\"    <string>{}</string>\", xml_escape(&config.device_token)),\n        format!(\"    <string>--timeout</string>\"),\n        format!(\"    <string>{}</string>\", config.timeout),\n    ];\n\n    if let Some(ref id) = config.node_id {\n        args.push(\"    <string>--node-id</string>\".to_string());\n        args.push(format!(\"    <string>{}</string>\", xml_escape(id)));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref name) = config.display_name {\n        args.push(\"    <string>--name</string>\".to_string());\n        args.push(format!(\"    <string>{}</string>\", xml_escape(name)));\n    }\n    if let Some(ref dir) = config.working_dir {\n        args.push(\"    <string>--working-dir</string>\".to_string());\n        args.push(format!(\"    <string>{}</string>\", xml_escape(dir)));\n    }"
}

> AGENT

The test expects cron sessions to have a delete button, but our change explicitly hides it for cron sessions. This looks like our change was intentional (from a previous merge resolution where we chose `!(isMain || isCron)`). But the test on main expects cron sessions to be deletable. We should revert to main's behavior — `!isMain` — since the test was written for that behavior and cron sessions should be deletable.

---
ID: PRRT_kwDOREW6tc52LThg
FILE: crates/swift-bridge/src/lib.rs:1728
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`_proxy_shutdown_tx` dropped before server exits**

`PreparedGateway` carries `_proxy_shutdown_tx` to keep the trusted-network proxy alive. Here, only `prepared.state` and `prepared.app` are moved out of `prepared`. The rest of `prepared` — including `_proxy_shutdown_tx` — is dropped at the end of the surrounding block, which is before `axum::serve` (running in a spawned task) ever completes.

Dropping the sender closes the watch channel, which is the proxy's shutdown signal, so the proxy terminates almost immediately after start-up.

The sender needs to be kept alive for the lifetime of the server task. The simplest fix is to retain it in a local variable that is `move`d into the spawn closure alongside `app`:

```rust
let app = prepared.app;
#[cfg(feature = "trusted-network")]
let _proxy_shutdown_tx = prepared._proxy_shutdown_tx;

BRIDGE.runtime.spawn(async move {
    #[cfg(feature = "trusted-network")]
    let _keep = _proxy_shutdown_tx; // holds the channel open
    let server = axum::serve(
        listener,
        app.into_make_service_with_connect_info::<std::net::SocketAddr>(),
    )
    ...
});
```


---
ID: PRRT_kwDOREW6tc52LTjP
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:1130
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Metrics history window silently shrinks to one-third**

The collection interval was changed from 30 s (gateway) to 10 s (httpd), but `max_points` in the in-memory ring-buffer is unchanged. Because the history load window is now computed as `max_points * 10` seconds, a ring-buffer sized for 720 points now covers:

- **Old (30 s interval):** 720 × 30 s = **6 hours**
- **New (10 s interval):** 720 × 10 s = **2 hours**

Any web-UI component, API client, or integration that expects to see more than 2 hours of metrics history will silently receive a shorter window. DB write frequency also triples, which may matter for embedded/low-power deployments.

If finer granularity is the goal, `max_points` (or the ring-buffer capacity in `GatewayState`) should be scaled proportionally (e.g. ×3 → 2160) to preserve the existing history window. Otherwise, the intent change should be documented.

---
ID: PRRT_kwDOREW6tc52LTk1
FILE: crates/gateway/src/server.rs:1076
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`cron_service.start()` contract not documented for non-HTTP consumers**

`PreparedGatewayCore` exports `cron_service` as a public field, but the cron scheduler only becomes active when `start()` is called — which currently happens inside `prepare_gateway` (httpd). Non-HTTP consumers (TUI, tests) that call `prepare_gateway_core` directly and receive a `PreparedGatewayCore` will silently have a dead scheduler unless they call `cron_service.start()` themselves.

Consider either:
- Starting the scheduler inside `prepare_gateway_core` (so callers don't have to), or
- Adding a doc comment to `cron_service` field noting that `CronService::start()` must be called before scheduled jobs execute.

---
ID: PRRT_kwDOREW6tc52MU_0
FILE: crates/node-host/src/service.rs:428
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`--host` and `--token` remain unquoted in `ExecStart`**

The PR correctly added quoting for `--node-id`, `--name`, and `--working-dir`, but the two newly-added required fields `gateway_url` and `device_token` are still interpolated without quotes. If a gateway URL ever contains a space (e.g. a URL encoded with unusual formatting), or if a device token has whitespace, systemd would silently split the value into multiple arguments, corrupting the command.

For consistency with the optional field handling and defense-in-depth:

```suggestion
    let mut exec_args = format!(
        "{bin} node run --host \"{}\" --token \"{}\" --timeout {}",
        config.gateway_url.replace('"', "\\\""),
        config.device_token.replace('"', "\\\""),
        config.timeout,
    );
```
```

## COMPREHENSIVE ASSESSMENT: HTTPD EXTRACTION COMPLETENESS

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's my detailed assessment:

### 1. **HTTP MODULES IN GATEWAY VS HTTPD**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅

All HTTP-specific modules have been correctly moved from `moltis-gateway` to `moltis-httpd`:

**Moved to httpd/src/:**
- `auth_middleware.rs` - HTTP auth enforcement via axum middleware
- `auth_routes.rs` - `/api/auth/*` endpoints 
- `env_routes.rs` - `/api/gon/env` endpoint
- `graphql_routes.rs` - GraphQL HTTP handler (feature-gated)
- `metrics_middleware.rs` - Metrics collection middleware (feature-gated)
- `metrics_routes.rs` - `/api/metrics/*` endpoints (feature-gated)
- `push_routes.rs` - Push notification HTTP endpoints (feature-gated)
- `request_throttle.rs` - HTTP request rate limiting
- `server.rs` - Core HTTP server setup, router building, AppState, axum integration
- `tailscale_routes.rs` - Tailscale auth/funnel routes (feature-gated)
- `tools_routes.rs` - `/api/tools/*` endpoints
- `upload_routes.rs` - File upload endpoints
- `ws.rs` - WebSocket upgrade and connection handling
- `channel_webhook_middleware.rs` - HTTP response mapping for webhook rejections

**Remaining in gateway/src/ (CORRECT):**
- `channel_webhook_middleware.rs` - Business logic for webhook signature verification (protocol-level, not HTTP-level). Uses `http::HeaderMap` which is appropriate.

### 2. **RE-EXPORTS AND IMPORT PATHS**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅

All consumers correctly import from the right crate:

**CLI (`crates/cli/`):**
- Imports `moltis_httpd::start_gateway()` ✅
- Imports `moltis_httpd::TailscaleOpts` ✅
- Imports `moltis_httpd::RouteEnhancer` ✅

**Web (`crates/web/`):**
- Imports UI utility functions from `moltis_gateway::server::openclaw_detected_for_ui()` ✅
- Imports UI utility functions from `moltis_gateway::server::local_llama_cpp_bytes_for_ui()` ✅
- These are correctly defined in gateway as they're UI utilities, not HTTP-specific

**Swift Bridge (`crates/swift-bridge/`):**
- Imports `moltis_httpd::prepare_httpd_embedded()` ✅
- Imports gateway types for core business logic ✅

**No incorrect imports found:**
- Zero instances of `moltis_gateway::AppState`, `moltis_gateway::PreparedGateway`, `moltis_gateway::build_gateway_app`, etc.
- Zero instances of `moltis_httpd` in gateway (correct - no circular dependency)

### 3. **DEPENDENCY MANAGEMENT**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅

**Gateway Cargo.toml:**
- ✅ Does NOT depend on `moltis-httpd`
- ✅ Does NOT depend on `axum`, `tower-http`, `rustls`, `tokio-tungstenite`
- ✅ Still depends on `http` (appropriate for `http::HeaderMap`)
- ✅ Properly forwards feature flags to httpd (graphql, push-notifications, slack, tailscale, tls, trusted-network, vault, web-ui)

**HTTPD Cargo.toml:**
- ✅ Depends on `moltis-gateway` ✅
- ✅ Depends on all HTTP-specific crates: `axum`, `tower-http`, `rustls`, `tokio-tungstenite` ✅
- ✅ Depends on `async-graphql` and `async-graphql-axum` (feature-gated) ✅
- ✅ All features properly guard HTTP-specific dependencies ✅

**CLI Cargo.toml:**
- ✅ Depends on both `moltis-gateway` and `moltis-httpd` ✅
- ✅ Feature mappings correctly route HTTP features to httpd:
  - `graphql` → `moltis-httpd/graphql`
  - `metrics` → `moltis-httpd/metrics`
  - `prometheus` → `moltis-httpd/prometheus`
  - `push-notifications` → `moltis-httpd/push-notifications`
  - `slack` → `moltis-httpd/slack`
  - `tailscale` → `moltis-httpd/tailscale`
  - `tls` → `moltis-httpd/tls`
  - `trusted-network` → `moltis-httpd/trusted-network`
  - `vault` → `moltis-httpd/vault`
  - `web-ui` → `moltis-httpd/web-ui`

### 4. **PUBLIC API EXPORTS**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅

**Gateway exports (business logic only):**
- `pub struct PreparedGatewayCore` - Core state container
- `pub async fn prepare_gateway_core()` - Initialize core business logic
- `pub fn approval_manager_from_config()` - Approval system setup
- `pub fn start_openclaw_background_tasks()` - Background task initialization
- `pub fn start_browser_warmup_after_listener()` - Browser warmup
- `pub fn openclaw_detected_for_ui()` - UI utility
- `pub fn local_llama_cpp_bytes_for_ui()` - UI utility

**HTTPD exports (HTTP layer):**
- `pub struct AppState` - HTTP application state
- `pub struct PreparedGateway` - HTTP-ready gateway
- `pub struct BannerMeta` - Startup banner metadata
- `pub struct TailscaleOpts` - Tailscale configuration
- `pub type RouteEnhancer` - Route enhancement callback
- `pub async fn prepare_gateway()` - Full HTTP gateway initialization
- `pub async fn prepare_gateway_embedded()` / `prepare_httpd_embedded()` - Embedded HTTP setup
- `pub async fn start_gateway()` - Start HTTP server
- `pub fn build_gateway_app()` - Build axum router
- `pub fn build_gateway_base()` - Build base routes
- `pub fn finalize_gateway_app()` - Finalize router
- `pub fn is_same_origin()` - CORS origin validation
- **Re-exports from gateway** (for consumer convenience):
  - `pub use moltis_gateway::server::openclaw_detected_for_ui`
  - `pub use prepare_gateway_embedded as prepare_httpd_embedded`

### 5. **CODE ORGANIZATION**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅

Clear separation of concerns:

**Gateway responsibilities (transport-agnostic):**
- Session management
- RPC method registry and dispatch
- Channel management and webhooks
- Chat service
- Agent/provider orchestration
- Approval system
- MCP service
- Cron scheduler
- Authentication (core logic, passwords, passkeys)

**HTTPD responsibilities (HTTP/WS transport):**
- Axum router construction
- HTTP middleware (auth, metrics, throttling)
- WebSocket connection handling
- HTTP route handlers
- CORS and origin validation
- TLS termination (delegated to gateway::server utilities for cert management)
- Tailscale serve/funnel integration
- GraphQL endpoint (wraps gateway methods via HTTP)

### 6. **NO TODO/FIXME COMMENTS**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅
- Zero TODO/FIXME comments related to httpd extraction, transport decoupling, or HTTP-specific migrations

### 7. **INTEGRATION POINTS**

**Status: COMPLETE** ✅

Critical integration paths:

1. **CLI → HTTPD → Gateway:**
   - CLI calls `moltis_httpd::start_gateway()`
   - HTTPD calls `moltis_gateway::server::prepare_gateway_core()`
   - Gateway initializes `PreparedGatewayCore`
   - HTTPD wraps in `PreparedGateway` with router

2. **Swift Bridge → HTTPD → Gateway:**
   - Swift calls `moltis_httpd::prepare_httpd_embedded()`
   - Same chain as above but managed by caller

3. **Web UI → Gateway:**
   - Templates call utility functions from `moltis_gateway::server`
   - These are data utilities, not HTTP-specific

### 8. **EDGE CASES AND NOTES**

**✅ All handled correctly:**

1. **`http::HeaderMap` in gateway:** Appropriate - it's protocol-level, not transport-level. Gateway needs this for webhook signature verification.

2. **Feature flag coordination:** When CLI enables `graphql`, it correctly enables both `moltis-httpd/graphql` AND `moltis-gateway/graphql` to ensure both HTTP and core logic can compile.

3. **Circular dependency protection:** Gateway has zero imports from httpd. One-way dependency: httpd → gateway.

4. **Function extraction:** Critical business logic utilities (openclaw detection, LLM memory reporting) correctly remain in gateway but are re-exported from httpd for consumer convenience.

5. **Tailscale handling:** Both crates properly feature-gate tailscale support independently. HTTPD routes are HTTP-specific, gateway modules are business-logic-specific.

### FINAL VERDICT

**The httpd extraction is COMPLETE and CORRECT.** ✅

All HTTP/transport-specific code has been cleanly extracted into `moltis-httpd`, while gateway retains only transport-agnostic business logic. The dependency graph is one-directional (httpd → gateway, never the reverse), all consumers import from the correct crate, and there are no remaining coupling issues.

**No further action required.** The separation is thorough and well-designed.

ID: PRRT_kwDOREW6tc52XZ6h
FILE: crates/skills/src/lib.rs:8
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **`install` module now always compiled despite no `install` feature**

The `#[cfg(feature = "install")]` guard was removed from `pub mod install`, but no `install` feature exists in `crates/skills/Cargo.toml`. This means the module was previously *never* compiled (the feature evaluated to `false` unconditionally) and is now permanently included in the public API surface of `moltis-skills`.

This changes the crate's public API for all downstream consumers and exposes `install_skill` and related helpers without an opt-in gate. If the intent is to always ship the install functionality, the dead `#[cfg]` gate was the only thing preventing that — but if other consumers (e.g. lightweight TUI builds) intentionally avoided compiling it, the change is unintentional.

Consider either:
- Adding a real `install` feature to `Cargo.toml` and re-applying the gate, or
- Explicitly documenting that the module is now always included.

```suggestion
pub mod install;
```
(If always-on is the intent, removing the gate is correct — but add a comment and `Cargo.toml` update to confirm this.)

---
ID: PRRT_kwDOREW6tc52XZ7s
FILE: crates/httpd/src/server.rs:1119
BODY: <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **History startup window uses wrong interval constant**

The history load window at startup is calculated as `max_points * 10` (line 1119) assuming a 10-second collection interval, but the actual `tokio::time::interval` at line 1170 fires every **30 seconds**:

```rust
let window_secs = max_points as u64 * 10; // 10-second intervals  ← wrong
// ...
let mut interval = tokio::time::interval(std::time::Duration::from_secs(30)); // 30 s
```

With the default 720-point ring-buffer, the gateway will only pre-load the last `720 × 10 = 7200 s ≈ 2 hours` from the DB instead of the expected `720 × 30 = 21600 s = 6 hours`. Any UI component expecting more than 2 hours of history on a fresh page load will receive a truncated dataset.

The cleanup counter comment on line 1269 is also wrong for the same reason: "360 ticks at 10s interval = 1 hour" actually fires every `360 × 30s = 3 hours`.

```suggestion
                let window_secs = max_points as u64 * 30; // 30-second intervals
```
```
